t\in(\tau_{m-1},\tau_{m}]
. We recall the error recursion evaluated outside of the reset events

e_{t}=\beta_{t}e_{t-1}+\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T}).

(34)

Multiplying this recursive relation by the
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable multiplier
A_{t}\coloneqq\prod_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{i}^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle A_{t}e_{t}

\displaystyle=A_{t}\beta_{t}e_{t-1}+A_{t}\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})

(35)

\displaystyle=A_{\tau_{m-1}}e_{\tau_{m-1}}+\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{j}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]+\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}\mathcal{Z}_{j}.

We define the masked stochastic sequence
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}\coloneqq\mathcal{Z}_{j}\mathbb{I}_{\{\tau_{m-1}<j\leq\tau_{m}\}}
. By Lemma
B.1
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
constitutes a valid
\mathcal{F}_{j}
-adapted martingale difference sequence.
Define the martingale
M_{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}A_{j}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
. Note that for all
t\in(\tau_{m-1},\tau_{m}]
, the sequence matches the localized stochastic accumulation
M_{t}=\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}\mathcal{Z}_{j}
. To apply Theorem
B.2
, we compute the variance proxy of the masked sequence. The variance proxy of
A_{j}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
evaluates to
A_{j}^{2}\Sigma_{j}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\{\tau_{m-1}<j\leq\tau_{m}\}}
, which implies the predictable variance proxy for
M_{t}
is
\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}^{2}\Sigma_{j}^{2}
. Consequently, invoking Theorem
B.2
, we establish that with probability at least
1-\delta/2
, the following implication holds

\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}\leq V_{t}\implies\|M_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2},\kappa\right)\sqrt{V_{t}},

(36)

where
C(\delta,\kappa)\coloneqq\sqrt{\kappa}+\sqrt{3\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}
, for any deterministic variance budget
V_{t}>0
.

To exploit this geometric concentration within the error dynamics, we manipulate (
35
) by multiplying both sides by
A_{t}^{-1}
and recognizing the algebraic identity
A_{t}^{-1}A_{j}=\Lambda_{t,j}\coloneqq\prod_{i=j+1}^{t}\beta_{i}
. This yields

e_{t}=\Lambda_{t,\tau_{m-1}}e_{\tau_{m-1}}+\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{j}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]+A_{t}^{-1}M_{t}.

(37)

Applying the triangle inequality, and acknowledging that the norm of the conditional bias
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{j}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]
is deterministically upper-bounded by
\mathcal{B}_{j}
per Assumption
4.1
, we deduce

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\Lambda_{t,\tau_{m-1}}\|e_{\tau_{m-1}}\|_{\mathcal{G}}+\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\mathcal{B}_{j}+A_{t}^{-1}\|M_{t}\|.

(38)

We have thus far established that with probability at least
1-\delta
, for a given epoch mapping
t\in(\tau_{m-1},\tau_{m}]
and a deterministic variance budget
V_{t}
, the condition
\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}\leq V_{t}
implies

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2},\kappa\right)\Lambda_{t,\tau_{m-1}}\sigma/\sqrt{B}+\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\mathcal{B}_{j}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2},\kappa\right)A_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{V_{t}}.

(39)

This probabilistic guarantee is currently localized to a specific realization of the random epoch
m
. To guarantee uniform convergence across the entire execution horizon
t\in\{1,\dots,T\}
, we must construct a union bound. Because there are at most
T
resets, a union bound over the maximum number of initialization events requires adjusting the confidence parameter to
\delta/T
to enforce the uniform guarantee

\forall m\in[T],\quad\|e_{\tau_{m-1}}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}.

(40)

For the martingale sequence constraint, we observe that the sequence of random stopping times
\{\tau_{m}\}_{m\geq 0}
forms a strict partition of the discrete temporal domain
\{1,\dots,T\}
into disjoint, contiguous sub-intervals
(\tau_{m-1},\tau_{m}]
. For any global time index
t\in\{1,\dots,T\}
, there exists one corresponding epoch index
m(t)
satisfying
t\in(\tau_{m(t)-1},\tau_{m(t)}]
. Therefore, across the entire trajectory of length
T
, the algorithm evaluates
T
unique localized martingales taking the explicit form
M_{t}=\sum_{j=\tau_{m(t)-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}\mathcal{Z}_{j}
. Because the cardinality of the set of all realized pairs
(m(t),t)
evaluates to
T
, applying the Union bound over all execution steps dictates refining the confidence parameter solely to
\delta/(2T)
. This structural combinatorial restriction formally circumvents the pessimistic
T^{2}
scaling that one may initially consider.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs for Instantiations of the Unified Estimation Error Bound

C.1 Propositions for Three Families with Zeroth / First / Second Order Corrections

The strength of Theorem
4.2
lies in its ability to recover distinct convergence mechanisms via specific choices of the control sequences
\{p_{t},\beta_{t},\eta_{t},\mathcal{T}_{t}\}
. In this section, we consider three special cases corresponding to the three families of estimators we discussed previously.

C.1
Family 1 with Zeroth-Order Correction.

Suppose Assumptions
2.3
,
3.1
,
3.2
, and
5.1
holds. Then, for the zeroth-order family,
\mathcal{B}_{t}=\beta_{t}\eta_{t-1}GL
and
\Sigma_{t}^{2}=(1-\beta_{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}.
Consequently, on any reset interval
(\tau_{m(t)-1},t]
, Theorem
4.2
yields

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\Lambda_{t,\tau_{m(t)-1}}\sigma/\sqrt{B}+LG\sum_{j=\tau_{m(t)-1}+1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\beta_{j}\eta_{j-1}+C\!\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)A_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{V_{t}},

(41)

whenever
\sum_{j=\tau_{m(t)-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}^{2}(1-\beta_{j})^{2}\sigma^{2}\leq V_{t}
.

Proposition
C.1
shows that the zeroth-order family attenuates the oracle noise by the factor
(1-\beta_{t})
, but this variance reduction comes at the cost of a tracking bias proportional to the step displacement. This is precisely the classical tradeoff in momentum-style estimation: larger momentum suppresses fresh noise, while simultaneously increasing the lag induced by the moving target.

C.2
Family 2 with First-Order Correction.

Suppose Assumptions
2.3
,
3.1
,
3.2
,
and
5.2
holds. Then, for the first-order family,
\mathcal{B}_{t}=0
and
\Sigma_{t}^{2}=2(1-\beta_{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta_{t}^{2}\ell^{2}\eta_{t-1}^{2}G^{2}.
Consequently, on any reset interval
(\tau_{m(t)-1},t]
, Theorem
4.2
yields

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\Lambda_{t,\tau_{m(t)-1}}\sigma/\sqrt{B}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)A_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{V_{t}},

(42)

whenever
\sum_{j=\tau_{m(t)-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}^{2}\Bigl(2(1-\beta_{j})^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta_{j}^{2}\ell^{2}\eta_{j-1}^{2}G^{2}\Bigr)\leq V_{t}.

Proposition
C.2
isolates the essential advantage of first-order differential estimators: the conditional bias disappears entirely. The price is that the variance term now depends on the centered stochastic difference, whose scale is proportional to the distance
\|w_{t}-w_{t-1}\|
. In other words, this family replaces tracking lag with local difference noise.

C.3
Family 3 with Second-Order Correction.

Suppose Assumptions
2.3
,
3.1
,
3.2
,

5.3
, and
5.4
hold. Then, for the second-order family,
\mathcal{B}_{t}=\frac{\alpha}{2}\beta_{t}\eta_{t-1}^{2}G^{2}
and
\Sigma_{t}^{2}=2(1-\beta_{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta_{t}^{2}\gamma^{2}\eta_{t-1}^{2}G^{2}.
Consequently, on any reset interval
(\tau_{m(t)-1},t]
, Theorem
4.2
yields

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\Lambda_{t,\tau_{m(t)-1}}\sigma/\sqrt{B}+\frac{\alpha G^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=\tau_{m(t)-1}+1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\beta_{j}\eta_{j-1}^{2}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)A_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{V_{t}},

(43)

whenever
\sum_{j=\tau_{m(t)-1}+1}^{t}A_{j}^{2}\Bigl(2(1-\beta_{j})^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta_{j}^{2}\gamma^{2}\eta_{j-1}^{2}G^{2}\Bigr)\leq V_{t}.

Proposition
C.3
makes explicit the gain obtained from using curvature information. Relative to the zeroth-order family, the deterministic bias is reduced from first order in the displacement to second order. Relative to the first-order family, the remaining stochastic fluctuation is now tied to Hessian-vector product noise rather than to stochastic differences. Thus, the second-order correction interpolates between low drift and curvature-sensitive variance control.

Before providing the proofs for the propositions of three families, we first state two facts which will be used in the proofs.

Fact 1.
Under Assumption
2.3
for
U_{t}
in the mirror descent
w_{t+1}=w_{t}-\eta_{t}P(w_{t},U_{t},\eta_{t})
, then
\|w_{t}-w_{t-1}\|\leq\eta_{t-1}G
is bounded almost surely.

Fact 2.
For the random vectors
X,Y\in\mathcal{G}
that admit variance proxies
\Sigma_{X}^{2},\Sigma_{Y}^{2}
such that
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}/\Sigma_{X}^{2})]\leq 2
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|Y\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}/\Sigma_{Y}^{2})]\leq 2
, then
X+Y
processes a variance proxy
2\Sigma_{X}^{2}+2\Sigma_{Y}^{2}
such that
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|X+Y\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}/(2\Sigma_{X}^{2}+2\Sigma_{Y}^{2}))]\leq 2
.

Fact 1 is established by noting
\|w_{t}-w_{t-1}\|=\|\eta_{t-1}P(w_{t-1},U_{t-1},\eta_{t-1})\|\leq\eta_{t-1}\|U_{t-1}\|_{*}
from Lemma
A.6
and
\|U_{t-1}\|_{*}\leq G
from Assumption
2.3
.

For fact 2, we establish a deterministic upper bound. For any two arbitrary, potentially dependent random vectors
X
and
Y
admitting variance proxies
\Sigma_{X}^{2}
and
\Sigma_{Y}^{2}
, the triangle inequality and the geometric inequality
\|X+Y\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\leq(\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}}+\|Y\|_{\mathcal{G}})^{2}\leq 2\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}+2\|Y\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
, paired with the convexity of the exponential function, dictates that

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\tfrac{\|X+Y\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}}{2\Sigma_{X}^{2}+2\Sigma_{Y}^{2}}\right)\right]\leq\tfrac{\Sigma_{X}^{2}}{\Sigma_{X}^{2}+\Sigma_{Y}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\tfrac{\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}}{\Sigma_{X}^{2}}\right)\right]+\tfrac{\Sigma_{Y}^{2}}{\Sigma_{X}^{2}+\Sigma_{Y}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\tfrac{\|Y\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}}{\Sigma_{Y}^{2}}\right)\right]\leq 2.

Consequently, the summation of two conditionally dependent sub-Gaussian vectors inherently possesses a variance proxy bounded by
2\Sigma_{X}^{2}+2\Sigma_{Y}^{2}
.

Proof of Proposition
C.1
.

Under the zeroth-order correction, the effective innovation becomes

\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})=(1-\beta_{t})\bigl(G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-g(w_{t})\bigr)-\beta_{t}\bigl(g(w_{t})-g(w_{t-1})\bigr).

Taking conditional expectation, using Assumption
5.1
, and noting fact 1
\|w_{t}-w_{t-1}\|\leq\eta_{t-1}G
under Assumption
2.3

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle=\beta_{t}\left\|\mathbb{E}[g(w_{t})-g(w_{t-1})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Jensen's}}{\leq}\beta_{t}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|g(w_{t})-g(w_{t-1})\|_{\mathcal{G}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{As.}\penalty\ \ref{ass:lipschitz}}{\leq}\beta_{t}L\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|w_{t}-w_{t-1}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]\leq\beta_{t}\eta_{t-1}GL=:\mathcal{B}_{t},

The centered innovation is

\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=(1-\beta_{t})\bigl(G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-g(w_{t})\bigr),

Assumption
4.1
implies
\Sigma_{t}^{2}=(1-\beta_{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}
. Substituting these quantities into Theorem
4.2
gives (
41
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
C.2
.

For
\mathcal{T}_{t}=0
, the effective innovation is

\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})=(1-\beta_{t})\bigl(G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-g(w_{t})\bigr)+\beta_{t}\Delta(w_{t},w_{t-1},\xi_{t}).

Both terms have zero conditional mean given
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
, so
\mathcal{B}_{t}=\left\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}=0.
It remains to control the centered innovation, which in this case equals
\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})
itself.

To construct the variance proxy
\Sigma_{t}^{2}
, we must account for the conditional dependence of the noise sources all evaluated at the same random realization
\xi_{t}
. Because these components share the same underlying random seed conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
, we cannot sum their variance proxies as if they were conditionally independent or orthogonal martingale sequences.

By applying the above fact 1 and fact 2 and utilizing the Assumption
2.3
, the variance proxy for
\mathcal{Z}_{t}
is bounded by

\Sigma_{t}^{2}=2(1-\beta_{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta_{t}^{2}\ell^{2}\eta_{t-1}^{2}G^{2}.

Substituting
\mathcal{B}_{t}=0
and this variance proxy into Theorem
4.2
gives (
42
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
C.3
.

Substituting the second-order correction into the effective innovation gives

\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})=(1-\beta_{t})\bigl(G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-g(w_{t})\bigr)+\beta_{t}\bigl(g(w_{t-1})-g(w_{t})-\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})(w_{t-1}-w_{t})\bigr).

Taking conditional expectation and using
\mathbb{E}[\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=\nabla g(w_{t})
, we obtain

\left\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}=\beta_{t}\|g(w_{t-1})-g(w_{t})-\nabla g(w_{t})(w_{t-1}-w_{t})\|_{\mathcal{G}}.

Assumption
5.4
yields the second-order Taylor remainder bound,
and noting fact 1 under Assumption
2.3
.

\left\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\beta_{t}\|w_{t}-w_{t-1}\|^{2}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\beta_{t}\eta_{t-1}^{2}G^{2}=:\mathcal{B}_{t}.

The centered innovation
\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
consequently distills into two purely stochastic components, representing the direct gradient evaluation noise and the error in the Hessian-vector product

\mathcal{Z}_{t}=(1-\beta_{t})(G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-g(w_{t}))-\beta_{t}(\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-\nabla g(w_{t}))(w_{t-1}-w_{t}).

By Assumption
5.3
and fact 1 under Assumption
2.3
, the Hessian-vector product noise evaluated along the update direction
w_{t-1}-w_{t}
is sub-Gaussian with a variance proxy bounded by
\gamma^{2}\|w_{t}-w_{t-1}\|^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}\eta_{t-1}^{2}G^{2}
. Summing the proxies and using our previous argument of fact 2, we ascertain the uniform predictable variance proxy for
\mathcal{Z}_{t}
as

\Sigma_{t}^{2}=2(1-\beta_{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta_{t}^{2}\gamma^{2}\eta_{t-1}^{2}G^{2}.

Substitution into Theorem
4.2
completes the proof.
∎

C.2 Zeroth-Order Correction (Standard Recursion and Resetting)

Recall that for the zeroth-order family, the correction map is defined as
\mathcal{T}_{t}(w_{t},w_{t-1},\xi_{t})\coloneqq\beta_{t}\big(G(w_{t-1},\xi_{t})-G(w_{t},\xi_{t})\big)
.

C.2.1 The case where
p_{t}=0
,
\beta_{t}=\beta
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

In this regime, the estimator is initialized with a large batch sample
G(w_{0},\xi_{0}^{B})
and the recursion proceeds deterministically as

v_{t}=(1-\beta)G(w_{t},\xi_{t})+\beta v_{t-1}.

(44)

The absence of stochastic resets implies the execution consists of a single continuous epoch where
m=1
,
\tau_{0}=0
, and
\tau_{1}=T
. Applying the constant parameters, the accumulated variance proxy evaluates to

\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}A_{j}^{2}\Sigma_{j}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}\beta^{-2j}(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}=(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}\frac{\beta^{-2t}-1}{1-\beta^{2}}.

(45)

We set the deterministic variance budget
V_{t}
to this exact bound. Utilizing the multiplier sequence
\Lambda_{t,\tau_{0}}=A_{t}^{-1}=\beta^{t}
, the cumulative deterministic bias is bounded by

\sum_{j=1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\mathcal{B}_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}LG\beta\eta\beta^{t-j}=LG\beta\eta\frac{1-\beta^{t}}{1-\beta}\leq\frac{LG\eta}{1-\beta}.

(46)

Simultaneously, by letting
V_{t}=\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}
, the scaled deviation radius simplifies to

A_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{V_{t}}=\beta^{t}(1-\beta)\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\beta^{-2t}-1}{1-\beta^{2}}}=\sigma\sqrt{\frac{(1-\beta)(1-\beta^{2t})}{1+\beta}}\leq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\leq\sigma\sqrt{1-\beta}.

(47)

Therefore, applying Theorem
4.2
, we establish that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the estimation error satisfies

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\beta^{t}C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+\frac{GL\eta}{1-\beta}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\sigma\sqrt{1-\beta}.

(48)

C.2.2 The case where
p_{t}=p
,
\beta_{t}=1
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

Under a probabilistic reset mechanism with full momentum, the estimator update takes the form

v_{t}=\begin{cases}G(w_{t},\xi_{t}^{B}),&\text{w.p. }p,\\
v_{t-1},&\text{w.p. }1-p.\end{cases}

(49)

The trajectory is partitioned into a random number of epochs, where the temporal length of each epoch is distributed geometrically with success probability
p
. Setting
\beta_{t}=1
enforces
A_{j}=1
and
\Lambda_{t,j}=1
universally. Furthermore, the variance proxy evaluates to
\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=0
, as the direct stochastic noise is fully suppressed by the deterministic momentum weight. Assigning
V_{t}=\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=0
, the cumulative bias inside the random epoch mapping to time
t
becomes the sole source of structural error:

\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\mathcal{B}_{j}=\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}LG\eta=LG\eta(t-\tau_{m-1})\leq LG\eta(\tau_{m}-\tau_{m-1}).

(50)

Let
E_{m}\coloneqq\tau_{m}-\tau_{m-1}
for all
m\in[T]
denote the duration of the
m
-th epoch. The maximum epoch length across the horizon is controlled via a standard union bound over the geometric tails

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m\in[T]}E_{m}\geq\mathfrak{E}_{max}\right)\leq\sum_{m=1}^{T}\mathbb{P}\left(E_{m}\geq\mathfrak{E}_{max}\right)=T(1-p)^{\mathfrak{E}_{max}}.

(51)

Enforcing the strict inequality
\log(1/(1-p))>p
, we constrain the failure probability to
\delta/4
, which yields
\mathfrak{E}_{max}\leq\frac{1}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}
. Integrating this maximum accumulation span, we deduce that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the estimation error respects the bound

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{4T},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+\frac{GL\eta}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}.

(52)

C.2.3 The case where
p_{t}=\mathbb{I}_{\{t\mod E=0\}}
,
\beta_{t}=1
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

Enforcing a deterministic periodic reset schedule restrains the maximum epoch length to
E
iterations. The estimator update evaluates as

v_{t}=\begin{cases}G(w_{t},\xi_{t}^{B}),&t\mod E=0,\\
v_{t-1},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(53)

Assuming the total horizon factors as
T=E\times N
for some positive integer
N
, the algorithm generates
N=T/E
deterministic epochs. Because the variance proxy
\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}
remains zero, by letting
V_{t}=\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=0
, the error is bounded entirely by the initialization noise and the maximum deterministic drift over the fixed interval
E
. Because the total number of reset nodes is reduced from
T
to
T/E
, the union bound covering the reset initializations requires a refined confidence adjustment of
\delta/(T/E)=\delta E/T
. Consequently, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the estimation error satisfies

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T/E},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+GL\eta E.

(54)

C.3 First-Order Correction (Differential Recursion and Resetting)

Recall that for this family of estimators, the correction term is set to zero, yielding
\mathcal{T}_{t}(w_{t},w_{t-1},\xi_{t})\coloneqq 0
. The effective innovation, therefore, consists exclusively of the direct noise and the difference noise, expressed as

\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})=(1-\beta_{t})(G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-g(w_{t}))+\beta_{t}\Delta(w_{t},w_{t-1},\xi_{t}).

(55)

C.3.1 The case where
p_{t}=0
,
\beta_{t}=\beta
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

In this regime, the estimator initializes with a large batch sample
G(w_{0},\xi_{0}^{B})
and processes deterministically without stochastic resets:

v_{t}=G(w_{t},\xi_{t})+\beta(v_{t-1}-G(w_{t-1},\xi_{t})).

(56)

Analogous to the zeroth-order family, the execution consists of a single continuous epoch where
m=1
,
\tau_{0}=0
, and
\tau_{1}=T
. Applying the constant parameters to the derived variance proxy, the accumulated proxy scales according to the predefined multipliers
A_{j}=\beta^{-j}
. The total variance proxy is evaluated as

\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}A_{j}^{2}\Sigma_{j}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}\beta^{-2j}\left(2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}\right).

(57)

Evaluating the geometric series, we define the deterministic variance budget
V_{t}
by letting
V_{t}=\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}

V_{t}=\left(2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}\right)\frac{\beta^{-2t}-1}{1-\beta^{2}}.

(58)

Because the systematic bias
\mathcal{B}_{j}
is zero, the cumulative bias component in the overarching theorem vanishes entirely, yielding
\sum_{j=1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\mathcal{B}_{j}=0
. The concentration term governed by the martingale difference relies on the modulated variance budget, which simplifies to

A_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{V_{t}}=\beta^{t}\sqrt{V_{t}}=\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta^{2t}}{1-\beta^{2}}}\sqrt{2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}}{1-\beta^{2}}}.

(59)

Invoking Theorem
4.2
we assert that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the estimation error satisfies

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\beta^{t}C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\sqrt{\frac{2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}}{1-\beta^{2}}}.

(60)

C.3.2 The case where
p_{t}=p
,
\beta_{t}=1
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

By assigning full weight to the differential update via
\beta_{t}=1
, the estimator replicates the behavior of the PAGE algorithm, resetting memory probabilistically with rate
p
:

v_{t}:=\begin{cases}G(w_{t},\xi_{t}^{B}),&w.p.\qquad p,\\
v_{t-1}+G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-G(w_{t-1},\xi_{t}),&w.p.\qquad 1-p,\end{cases}

(61)

The explicit choice of full momentum inherently nullifies the direct noise variance contribution, leaving the variance proxy dependent on the geometric continuity of the gradient space. Substituting
\beta_{t}=1
yields a constant variance proxy uniformly bounded by
\Sigma_{j}^{2}\leq 2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}
. The corresponding multipliers resolve to unity (
A_{j}=1
and
\Lambda_{t,j}=1
), dictating that the variance accumulation grows linearly with the duration of the random epoch
E_{m}\coloneqq\tau_{m}-\tau_{m-1}
. The accumulated variance proxy inside the epoch thus evaluates to

\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}=2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}(t-\tau_{m-1})\leq 2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}E_{m}.

(62)

As established previously, the maximal epoch length
\mathfrak{E}_{max}
governed by the underlying geometric distribution satisfies
\mathfrak{E}_{max}\leq\frac{1}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}
with high probability. Setting the deterministic variance budget
V_{t}
to
2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}\frac{1}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}
, and recalling the zero-bias condition
\mathcal{B}_{t}=0
, the structure of the error bound eliminates the linear growth associated with deterministic drift. For this configuration, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the terminal estimation error is subsequently bounded by

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{4T},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{4T},\kappa\right)\ell\eta G\sqrt{\frac{2}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}}.

(63)

C.3.3 The case where
p_{t}=\mathbb{I}_{\{t\mod E=0\}}
,
\beta_{t}=1
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

Under a cyclic deterministic reset mechanism, the temporal length of each estimation epoch is constrained to
E
iterations:

v_{t}:=\begin{cases}G(w_{t},\xi_{t}^{B}),&t\mod E=0,\\
v_{t-1}+G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-G(w_{t-1},\xi_{t}),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(64)

Assuming the horizon
T=E\times N
aligns perfectly with these periodic boundaries, the stochastic behavior is constrained similarly to the probabilistic reset case, yet enforces deterministic termination bounds for each tracking segment. Given
\beta_{t}=1
, the variance proxy bound simplifies to
\Sigma_{j}^{2}\leq 2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}
. Because the length of the interval
t-\tau_{m-1}
is deterministically upper-bounded by
E
, the variance budget across any arbitrary epoch deterministically accumulates to

\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}\leq 2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}E.

(65)

Setting the strict variance budget
V_{t}=2\ell^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}E
and combining this with the intrinsic zero-bias property characterizing this estimator family, we embed these components into the unified framework. Consequently, incorporating the deterministic epoch structure and applying the appropriate uniform bounds over the respective intervals, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the estimation error satisfies

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T/E},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\ell\eta G\sqrt{2E}.

(66)

C.4 Second-Order Correction (Second-Order Recursion and Resetting)

Recall the explicit formulation of the second-order correction mechanism designed to correct the drift using local curvature, given by
\mathcal{T}_{t}(w_{t},w_{t-1},\xi_{t})\coloneqq\beta_{t}\Big(G(w_{t-1},\xi_{t})-\big[G(w_{t},\xi_{t})+\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})(w_{t-1}-w_{t})\big]\Big)
. Substituting this correction into the general effective innovation recursion, we obtain

\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathcal{T})=(1-\beta_{t})(G(w_{t},\xi_{t})-g(w_{t}))+\beta_{t}\big(g(w_{t-1})-g(w_{t})-\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})(w_{t-1}-w_{t})\big).

(67)

C.4.1 The case where
p_{t}=0
,
\beta_{t}=\beta
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

Under a purely recursive update, absent random resets, the process evolves across a single continuous epoch:

v_{t}:=(1-\beta_{t})G(w_{t},\xi_{t})+\beta_{t}\Big(v_{t-1}-\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})(w_{t-1}-w_{t})\Big).

(68)

Imposing the fixed hyperparameters onto the variance framework, we establish the accumulated variance proxy utilizing the geometric sequence
A_{j}=\beta^{-j}
. The proxy integral evaluates to

\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}A_{j}^{2}\Sigma_{j}^{2}=\left(2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}\right)\frac{\beta^{-2t}-1}{1-\beta^{2}}.

(69)

Setting the deterministic variance budget
V_{t}=\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}
to this quantity, we concurrently evaluate the cumulative deterministic bias induced by the Taylor remainder. The bias accumulates through the discounted sum defined by
\Lambda_{t,j}=\beta^{t-j}
, leading to

\sum_{j=1}^{t}\Lambda_{t,j}\mathcal{B}_{j}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{t}\beta^{t-j}\frac{\alpha}{2}\beta\eta^{2}G^{2}=\frac{\alpha}{2}\beta\eta^{2}G^{2}\frac{1-\beta^{t}}{1-\beta}\leq\frac{\alpha\beta\eta^{2}G^{2}}{2(1-\beta)}.

(70)

Isolating the modulated variance term within the martingale concentration mechanism yields the upper bound
A_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{V_{t}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}}{1-\beta^{2}}}
. Applying Theorem
4.2
over this singular deterministic epoch, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the estimation error satisfies

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\beta^{t}C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+\frac{\alpha\beta\eta^{2}G^{2}}{2(1-\beta)}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\sqrt{\frac{2(1-\beta)^{2}\sigma^{2}+2\beta^{2}\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}}{1-\beta^{2}}}.

(71)

C.4.2 The case where
p_{t}=p
,
\beta_{t}=1
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

Setting the momentum uniformly to 1 transitions the estimator into a differential tracker that leverages stochastic second-order corrections, accompanied by memory resets distributed geometrically with rate
p
:

v_{t}:=\begin{cases}G(w_{t},\xi_{t}^{B}),&w.p.\qquad p,\\
v_{t-1}-\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})(w_{t-1}-w_{t}),&w.p.\qquad 1-p.\end{cases}

(72)

Because
\beta_{t}=1
, the direct noise term evaluates to zero, coupling the variance proxy to the sub-Gaussian Hessian noise such that
\Sigma_{j}^{2}=2\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}
. Similarly, the per-step deterministic bias stabilizes at
\mathcal{B}_{j}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}
. Due to
A_{j}=1
and
\Lambda_{t,j}=1
, the accumulation of both the bias and the variance proxy scales linearly with the duration of the current random epoch
E_{m}\coloneqq\tau_{m}-\tau_{m-1}
. Within this epoch, the summations are bounded by

\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}\mathcal{B}_{j}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}E_{m}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}=\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}\Sigma_{j}^{2}\leq 2\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}E_{m}.

(73)

Invoking the high-probability uniform bound on the maximum epoch length, we verify
\mathfrak{E}_{max}\leq\frac{1}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}
. By defining the deterministic variance budget as
V_{t}=2\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}\frac{1}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}
, and bounding the total bias over the longest possible epoch, we assemble the complete error topology. Consequently, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the terminal estimation error is bounded by

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{4T},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+\frac{\alpha\eta^{2}G^{2}}{2p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{4T},\kappa\right)\gamma\eta G\sqrt{\frac{2}{p}\log\frac{4T}{\delta}}.

(74)

C.4.3 The case where
p_{t}=\mathbb{I}_{\{t\mod E=0\}}
,
\beta_{t}=1
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta
deterministically

Imposing a deterministic, periodic reset schedule ensures that memory truncation occurs every
E
iterations:

v_{t}:=\begin{cases}G(w_{t},\xi_{t}^{B}),&t\mod E=0,\\
v_{t-1}-\nabla G(w_{t},\xi_{t})(w_{t-1}-w_{t}),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(75)

Assuming the computational horizon is an integer multiple of the epoch length,
T=E\times N
, this architecture completely arrests the unbounded accumulation of estimation lag while preserving the full corrective weight
\beta_{t}=1
. The fundamental quantities parallel the probabilistic setting, maintaining
\Sigma_{j}^{2}=2\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}
and
\mathcal{B}_{j}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}
. However, the interval lengths are now deterministically capped at
t-\tau_{m-1}\leq E
. The cumulative proxy and bias are correspondingly bounded across the fixed interval

\sum_{j=\tau_{m-1}+1}^{t}\mathcal{B}_{j}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}E\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{2}\leq 2\gamma^{2}\eta^{2}G^{2}E.

(76)

Assigning this upper bound to
V_{t}
and directly incorporating the maximal deterministic bias contribution, the bounds concentrate without the logarithmic geometric penalties. We establish that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the resulting error magnitude honors the bound

\|e_{t}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T/E},\kappa\right)\sigma/\sqrt{B}+\frac{\alpha\eta^{2}G^{2}E}{2}+C\left(\frac{\delta}{2T},\kappa\right)\gamma\eta G\sqrt{2E}.

(77)

C.5 Examples for Assumption
5.2
of Sub-Gaussian Lipschitzness of the Centered Differences

The sub-Gaussian tail assumptions utilized in our analysis are automatically satisfied in standard Empirical Risk Minimization (ERM) configurations. Let the function (or the gradient) be defined over a finite dataset as
g(w)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{i}(w)
, where the stochastic oracle samples a component index uniformly at random. If every individual component
G_{i}
is
L_{i}
-Lipschitz continuous, the discrete nature of the sample space guarantees that the centered difference noise is bounded almost surely by
2\max_{i}L_{i}\|w-w^{\prime}\|
. Because any bounded random variable is sub-Gaussian, Assumption
5.2
is globally satisfied with the variance proxy parameter
\ell=2\max_{i}L_{i}
. Nonetheless, we present concrete instantiations satisfying Assumption
5.2
next.

We leverage the moment-generating function of a scaled chi-squared distribution: for a standard Gaussian random variable
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, the expectation
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda Z^{2})]=(1-2\lambda)^{-1/2}
is valid for all
\lambda<1/2
. Enforcing the condition
(1-2\lambda)^{-1/2}\leq 2
necessitates
\lambda\leq\frac{3}{8}
.

Recall that Assumption
5.2
requires the centered estimate difference noise
\Delta(w,w^{\prime},\xi)\coloneqq(G(w,\xi)-G(w^{\prime},\xi))-(g(w)-g(w^{\prime}))
to concentrate with a variance proxy bounded by
\ell^{2}\|w-w^{\prime}\|^{2}
.

Example 1: Scalar Function Configuration.

Let the primal space
\mathcal{X}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}
be equipped with the Euclidean geometry
\|\cdot\|:=\|\cdot\|_{2}
. Consider a stochastic linear observation model where the oracle evaluates a scalar function
G:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega\to\mathcal{G}:=\mathbb{R}
with
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{G}}:=|\cdot|
defined by

G(w,\xi)=\langle\xi,w\rangle,

(78)

where
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)
is a centered Gaussian random vector. The population function is zero,
g(w)=\mathbb{E}[G(w,\xi)]=0
. The centered difference evaluates to the inner product

\Delta(w,w^{\prime},\xi)=\langle\xi,w-w^{\prime}\rangle.

(79)

For any fixed deterministic
w
and
w^{\prime}
, the random variable
\Delta
is a univariate Gaussian distributed as
\mathcal{N}(0,(w-w^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma(w-w^{\prime}))
. The variance is bounded by the spectral norm,
\nu^{2}\coloneqq(w-w^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma(w-w^{\prime})\leq\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|w-w^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}
. Letting
Z=\Delta/\nu\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, we verify the condition

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{|\Delta(w,w^{\prime},\xi)|^{2}}{\ell^{2}\|w-w^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\ell^{2}}Z^{2}\right)\right].

(80)

By setting
\lambda=\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}/\ell^{2}
, the sub-Gaussian Lipschitz condition is satisfied for any structural constant satisfying
\ell\geq\sqrt{8\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}/3}
.

Example 2: Vector Field Configuration (Gradient of a Potential).

Consider a stochastic quadratic objective
F:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
with the primal space
(\mathcal{X},\|\cdot\|):=(\mathbb{R}^{d},\|\cdot\|_{2})
defined by

F(w,\xi)=\frac{1}{2}w^{\top}(A+\xi I)w,

(81)

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is a deterministic symmetric matrix, and
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
is a scalar Gaussian noise perturbing the curvature. The stochastic vector field is the gradient
G(w,\xi)=\nabla_{w}F(w,\xi)=(A+\xi I)w
, implicitly setting
\mathcal{G}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{G}}:=\|\cdot\|_{2}
, making the population gradient
g(w)=Aw
. The centered estimate difference noise simplifies algebraically to

\Delta(w,w^{\prime},\xi)=\xi(w-w^{\prime}).

(82)

Taking the squared dual norm (which coincides with the
\ell_{2}
norm), we obtain
\|\Delta(w,w^{\prime},\xi)\|_{2}^{2}=\xi^{2}\|w-w^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}
. The exponential moment evaluates as

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\|\Delta(w,w^{\prime},\xi)\|_{2}^{2}}{\ell^{2}\|w-w^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\left(\frac{\xi}{\sigma}\right)^{2}\right)\right].

(83)

Recognizing
(\xi/\sigma)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, the condition holds globally provided
\ell\geq\sigma\sqrt{8/3}
.

Appendix D
Lemmas in Proofs for Two Applications

D.1
Bound for Proximal Gradient Mapping with Normalized Direction, see similar Lemma 2 in
[
31
]
.

Suppose
w\in\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
,
u,\delta\in\mathcal{X}^{*}
,
\eta>0
, and

\|u\|_{\ast}\neq 0,\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}\neq 0
.
Then for the proximal gradient mapping
P
defined in Definition
2.2
, we have:

\left\langle u,P\left(w,\frac{u+\delta}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}},\eta\right)\right\rangle\geq\frac{1}{4}\left\langle u,P\left(w,\frac{u}{\|u\|_{\ast}},\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\right\rangle-2\|\delta\|_{\ast}

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

Noting the scaling property of the proximal gradient mapping (Lemma
A.6
)
such that
P(w,\alpha u,\eta)=\alpha P(w,u,\alpha\eta),\forall\alpha>0
, we have:

P\left(w,\frac{u+\delta}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}},\eta\right)=\frac{P(w,u+\delta,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}},\quad P\left(w,\frac{u}{\|u\|_{\ast}},\frac{\eta}{2}\right)=\frac{P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))}{\|u\|_{\ast}}

Therefore, the desired inequality to be proved is equivalent to:

-\frac{\left\langle u,P(w,u+\delta,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})\right\rangle}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}\leq-\frac{\left\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))\right\rangle}{4\|u\|_{\ast}}+2\|\delta\|_{\ast}

Now we start to prove the above equivalent inequality, then the desired inequality will follow by noting the scaling property of the proximal gradient mapping.
We consider the following two cases for the ease of analysis:
(1)
\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
; (2)
\|\delta\|_{\ast}>\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
.

Case (1):

\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
.

Applying the properties of the proximal gradient mapping (Lemma
A.6
)
such that
\|P(w,u_{1},\eta)-P(w,u_{2},\eta)\|\leq\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\ast}
,
and
\langle\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\rangle\leq\|\mathbf{x}\|_{\ast}\|\mathbf{y}\|
for any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{\ast},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have:

-\langle u,P(w,u+\delta,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})\rangle\leq-\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})\rangle+\|u\|_{\ast}\|\delta\|_{\ast}

By using the condition
\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
and the triangle inequality, we have:

\frac{2}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}\leq\|u\|_{\ast}-\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\|u\|_{\ast}+\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{4}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}

Noting the monotonicity of the proximal gradient mapping (Lemma
A.6
)

\langle u,\eta^{\prime}P(w,u,\eta^{\prime})\rangle\geq\langle u,\eta P(w,u,\eta)\rangle\geq 0
for any
\eta^{\prime}\geq\eta>0
,
and using the condition
\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
:

\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})\rangle\geq\frac{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}{2\|u\|_{\ast}}\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))\rangle\geq\frac{1}{3}\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))\rangle

By combining the above three inequalities, we have the desired inequality for Case (1):
\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
.

\left[-\frac{\langle u,P(w,u+\delta,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})\rangle}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}\right]\mathds{1}_{\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}}\leq\left[-\frac{\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))\rangle}{4\|u\|_{\ast}}+2\|\delta\|_{\ast}\right]\mathds{1}_{\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}}

Case (2):

\|\delta\|_{\ast}>\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
.

Applying the properties of the proximal gradient mapping (Lemma
A.6
)
such that
\langle u,P(w,u,\eta)\rangle\geq\|P(w,u,\eta)\|^{2}\geq 0
,
\|P(w,u,\eta)\|\leq\|u\|_{\ast}
,
and using
\langle\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\rangle\leq\|\mathbf{x}\|_{\ast}\|\mathbf{y}\|
for any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{\ast},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{X}
:

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle u,P(w,u+\delta,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})\rangle}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle u+\delta,P(w,u+\delta,\frac{\eta}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}})\rangle}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}+\frac{\langle\delta,P(w,u+\delta,\frac{\eta}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}})\rangle}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0+\frac{\|\delta\|_{\ast}\|P(w,u+\delta,\frac{\eta}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}})\|}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}\leq\|\delta\|_{\ast}

Given that condition
\|\delta\|_{\ast}>\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
,
and using the properties of the proximal gradient mapping (Lemma
A.6
)
such that
\langle u,P(w,u,\eta)\rangle\leq\|u\|_{\ast}\|P(w,u,\eta)\|,\|P(w,u,\eta)\|\leq\|u\|_{\ast}
, we have:

\frac{\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))\rangle}{4\|u\|_{\ast}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))\|\leq\frac{1}{4}\|u\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}<\|\delta\|_{\ast}

Combining the above two inequalities, we have the desired inequality for Case (2):
\|\delta\|_{\ast}>\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}
.

\left[-\frac{\langle u,P(w,u+\delta,\eta/\|u+\delta\|_{\ast})\rangle}{\|u+\delta\|_{\ast}}\right]\mathds{1}_{\|\delta\|_{\ast}>\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}}\leq\left[-\frac{\langle u,P(w,u,\eta/(2\|u\|_{\ast}))\rangle}{4\|u\|_{\ast}}+2\|\delta\|_{\ast}\right]\mathds{1}_{\|\delta\|_{\ast}>\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}}

Summing up the two inequalities for these
two cases
and noting that
1=\mathds{1}_{\|\delta\|_{\ast}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}}+\mathds{1}_{\|\delta\|_{\ast}>\frac{1}{3}\|u\|_{\ast}}
,
we complete the proof of the equivalent inequality, and the desired inequality follows. ∎

D.2
Optimal Decoupled Bounds with Selection of Step Size and Momentum Parameter.

For constants
C_{1},\dots,C_{6}\geq 0
, and
C_{2}C_{4}C_{5}C_{6}\neq 0
, and horizon
T\geq 1
, define the objective functions:

\displaystyle f_{1}(\eta,1-\beta)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+\frac{C_{3}}{(1-\beta)T}+C_{4}\sqrt{1-\beta}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{1-\beta}

\displaystyle f_{2}(\eta,1-\beta)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+\frac{C_{3}}{(1-\beta)T}+C_{4}\sqrt{1-\beta}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{\sqrt{1-\beta}}

\displaystyle f_{3}(\eta,1-\beta)

\displaystyle=f_{2}(\eta,1-\beta)+\frac{C_{6}\eta^{2}}{1-\beta}

Letting
\eta=\min\left\{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{-\frac{1}{2}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}^{3}}{C_{4}^{2}C_{5}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}T^{-\frac{3}{4}}\right\},1-\beta=\max\left\{\left(\frac{C_{3}}{C_{4}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\}

f_{1}(\eta,1-\beta)\leq 2\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\left(\frac{C_{3}C_{4}^{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+3\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{4}^{2}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\,\textbf{(Family 1)}

when
T\geq\max\left\{\frac{C_{3}}{C_{4}},\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}\right\}
.

Letting
\eta=\min\left\{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{-\frac{1}{2}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{C_{4}C_{5}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}}\right\},1-\beta=\max\left\{\left(\frac{C_{3}}{C_{4}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}}\right\}

f_{2}(\eta,1-\beta)\leq 2\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\left(\frac{C_{3}C_{4}^{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+3\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{4}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\,\textbf{(Family 2)}

when
T\geq\max\left\{\frac{C_{3}}{C_{4}},\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}\right\}
.

Letting
\eta=\min\left\{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{-\frac{1}{2}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{C_{4}C_{5}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}^{3}}{C_{4}^{2}C_{6}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}T^{-\frac{3}{5}}\right\}
,

1-\beta=\max\left\{\left(\frac{C_{3}}{C_{4}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\;\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{6}}{C_{4}^{3}}\right)^{\frac{2}{5}}T^{-\frac{4}{5}}\right\}

f_{3}(\eta,1-\beta)\leq 2\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\left(\frac{C_{3}C_{4}^{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+3\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{4}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+3\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{4}^{2}C_{6}}{T^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}\,\textbf{(Family 3)}

when
T\geq\max\left\{\frac{C_{3}}{C_{4}},\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}},\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{6}}{C_{4}^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}
.

Proof.

To simplify the analysis, we introduce
y=1-\beta
. We denote the fundamental structural limits present in the bounds as:

L_{1}=\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},L_{2}=\left(\frac{C_{3}C_{4}^{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},L_{3}=\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{4}^{2}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}},L_{4}=\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{4}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},L_{5}=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{4}^{2}C_{6}}{T^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}

We define the unscaled parameter candidate blocks present in the Lemma selections as follows:

\eta_{A}=\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{-\frac{1}{2}},\,\eta_{B}=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{3}}{C_{4}^{2}C_{5}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}T^{-\frac{3}{4}},\,\eta_{C}=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{C_{4}C_{5}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\,\eta_{D}=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{3}}{C_{4}^{2}C_{6}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}T^{-\frac{3}{5}}

y_{A}=\left(\frac{C_{3}}{C_{4}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\,y_{B}=\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{-\frac{1}{2}},\,y_{C}=\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{-\frac{2}{3}},\,y_{D}=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{6}}{C_{4}^{3}}\right)^{\frac{2}{5}}T^{-\frac{4}{5}}

For
Family 1
, substituting
\eta=\min\{\eta_{A},\eta_{B}\}
and
y=\max\{y_{A},y_{B}\}
into the objective function
and using
\eta\leq\eta_{A},\frac{1}{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{A}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{B}},\sqrt{y}\leq\sqrt{y_{A}}+\sqrt{y_{B}},\frac{1}{y}\leq\frac{1}{y_{A}},\frac{\eta}{y}\leq\frac{\eta_{B}}{y_{B}}

yields the following inequality:

\displaystyle f_{1}(\eta,1-\beta)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+\frac{C_{3}}{yT}+C_{4}\sqrt{y}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{y}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{B}T}\right)+C_{2}\eta_{A}+\frac{C_{3}}{y_{A}T}+C_{4}\left(\sqrt{y_{A}}+\sqrt{y_{B}}\right)+\frac{C_{5}\eta_{B}}{y_{B}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+C_{2}\eta_{A}\right)}_{2L_{1}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{3}}{y_{A}T}+C_{4}\sqrt{y_{A}}\right)}_{2L_{2}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{B}T}+C_{4}\sqrt{y_{B}}+\frac{C_{5}\eta_{B}}{y_{B}}\right)}_{3L_{3}}

\displaystyle=2L_{1}+2L_{2}+3L_{3}.

For
Family 2
, substituting
\eta=\min\{\eta_{A},\eta_{C}\}
and
y=\max\{y_{A},y_{C}\}

and using
\eta\leq\eta_{A},\frac{1}{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{A}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{C}},\sqrt{y}\leq\sqrt{y_{A}}+\sqrt{y_{C}},\frac{1}{y}\leq\frac{1}{y_{A}},\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{y}}\leq\frac{\eta_{C}}{\sqrt{y_{C}}}

yields the following inequality:

\displaystyle f_{2}(\eta,1-\beta)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+\frac{C_{3}}{yT}+C_{4}\sqrt{y}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{\sqrt{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{C}T}\right)+C_{2}\eta_{A}+\frac{C_{3}}{y_{A}T}+C_{4}\left(\sqrt{y_{A}}+\sqrt{y_{C}}\right)+\frac{C_{5}\eta_{C}}{\sqrt{y_{C}}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+C_{2}\eta_{A}\right)}_{2L_{1}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{3}}{y_{A}T}+C_{4}\sqrt{y_{A}}\right)}_{2L_{2}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{C}T}+C_{4}\sqrt{y_{C}}+\frac{C_{5}\eta_{C}}{\sqrt{y_{C}}}\right)}_{3L_{4}}

\displaystyle=2L_{1}+2L_{2}+3L_{4}.

For
Family 3
, substituting
\eta=\min\{\eta_{A},\eta_{C},\eta_{D}\}
and
y=\max\{y_{A},y_{C},y_{D}\}

and using
\eta\leq\eta_{A},\frac{1}{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{A}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{C}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{D}},\sqrt{y}\leq\sqrt{y_{A}}+\sqrt{y_{C}}+\sqrt{y_{D}},\frac{1}{y}\leq\frac{1}{y_{A}},\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{y}}\leq\frac{\eta_{C}}{\sqrt{y_{C}}},\frac{\eta^{2}}{y}\leq\frac{\eta_{D}^{2}}{y_{D}}

extends the previous inequality by incorporating the quadratic term:

\displaystyle f_{3}(\eta,1-\beta)

\displaystyle=f_{2}(\eta,1-\beta)+\frac{C_{6}\eta^{2}}{y}

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{1}+2L_{2}+3L_{4}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{D}T}+C_{4}\sqrt{y_{D}}+\frac{C_{6}\eta_{D}^{2}}{y_{D}}\right)}_{3L_{5}}

\displaystyle=2L_{1}+2L_{2}+3L_{4}+3L_{5}.

This strictly recovers the bounds stated in the Lemma.
∎

D.3
Optimal Bound with Selection of Step Size, Reset Probability and Batch Size.

Let
B,E,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
p=\frac{1}{E}\in(0,1]
,
\eta>0
. For
C_{1},\ldots,C_{6}\geq 0
with
C_{1}C_{2}C_{4}C_{5}C_{6}\neq 0
and
C_{3}=0
, define the objective functions as follows:

\displaystyle f_{1}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+0+C_{4}\sqrt{p}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{p}

\displaystyle f_{2}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+0+C_{4}\sqrt{p}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{\sqrt{p}}

\displaystyle f_{3}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}

\displaystyle=f_{2}(\eta,p)+\frac{C_{6}\eta^{2}}{p}

Letting
\eta=\min\left\{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\left(\frac{2C_{1}^{3}}{C_{4}^{2}C_{5}T^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}

and
p=\frac{1}{E}
with
B=E=\left\lceil\left(\frac{C_{4}^{2}T}{8C_{1}C_{5}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\rceil
,
when
T\geq\frac{8C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}

f_{1}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}\leq 2\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+4\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{4}^{2}C_{5}}{2T}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\,\textbf{(Family 1)}

Letting
\eta=\min\left\{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{\sqrt{2}C_{4}C_{5}T^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right\}

and
p=\frac{1}{E}
with
B=E=\left\lceil\left(\frac{C_{4}^{4}T^{2}}{2C_{1}^{2}C_{5}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right\rceil
,
when
T\geq\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}

f_{2}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}\leq 2\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+3\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{1}C_{4}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\,\textbf{(Family 2)}

Letting
\eta=\min\left\{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{\sqrt{2}C_{4}C_{5}T^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},\left(\frac{C_{1}^{3}}{4C_{4}^{2}C_{6}T^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}\right\}

and
p=\frac{1}{E}
with
B=E=\left\lceil\min\left\{\left(\frac{C_{4}^{4}T^{2}}{2C_{1}^{2}C_{5}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},\left(\frac{C_{4}^{6}T^{4}}{16C_{1}^{4}C_{6}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}\right\}\right\rceil
,
when
T\geq\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}},2\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{6}}{C_{4}^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}

f_{3}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}\leq 2\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+3\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{1}C_{4}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+5\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{4}^{2}C_{6}}{8T^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}\,\textbf{(Family 3)}

Proof.

Before establishing the bounds, we define the following variables
\eta_{A},\eta_{B},\eta_{C},\eta_{D}
, the continuous batch size roots
E_{B},E_{C},E_{D}
, and the AM-GM minimal values
M_{1},M_{2},M_{3},M_{4}
:

\displaystyle\eta_{A}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle M_{1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\eta_{B}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2C_{1}^{3}}{C_{4}^{2}C_{5}T^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle E_{B}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{4}^{2}T}{8C_{1}C_{5}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle M_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{4}^{2}C_{5}}{2T}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\eta_{C}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{\sqrt{2}C_{4}C_{5}T^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle E_{C}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{4}^{4}T^{2}}{2C_{1}^{2}C_{5}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle M_{3}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{1}C_{4}C_{5}}{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\eta_{D}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{3}}{4C_{4}^{2}C_{6}T^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}

\displaystyle E_{D}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{4}^{6}T^{4}}{16C_{1}^{4}C_{6}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}

\displaystyle M_{4}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{4}^{2}C_{6}}{8T^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}

For
Family 1
, substituting
\eta=\min\{\eta_{A},\eta_{B}\},p=\frac{1}{E}=\frac{1}{\lceil E_{B}\rceil}
,
and noting that
E_{B}\geq 1
when
T\geq\frac{8C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}
therefore
E_{B}\leq\lceil E_{B}\rceil<E_{B}+1\leq 2E_{B}
,
and using
\eta\leq\eta_{A},\frac{1}{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{A}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{B}},\sqrt{p}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{E}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{E_{B}}},\frac{\eta}{p}\leq\eta_{B}\cdot 2E_{B}
,
we have:

\displaystyle f_{1}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+0+C_{4}\sqrt{p}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{B}T}\right)+C_{2}\eta_{A}+0+\frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{E_{B}}}+2C_{5}\eta_{B}E_{B}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+C_{2}\eta_{A}\right)}_{2M_{1}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{B}T}+\frac{C_{4}/2}{\sqrt{E_{B}}}+\frac{C_{4}/2}{\sqrt{E_{B}}}+2C_{5}\eta_{B}E_{B}\right)}_{4M_{2}}

\displaystyle=2M_{1}+4M_{2}.

For
Family 2
, substituting
\eta=\min\{\eta_{A},\eta_{C}\},p=\frac{1}{E}=\frac{1}{\lceil E_{C}\rceil}
,
and noting that
E_{C}\geq 1
when
T\geq\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}}
therefore
E_{C}\leq\lceil E_{C}\rceil<E_{C}+1\leq 2E_{C}
,
and using
\eta\leq\eta_{A},\frac{1}{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{A}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{C}},\sqrt{p}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{E}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{E_{C}}},\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{p}}\leq\eta_{C}\cdot\sqrt{2E_{C}}
,
we have:

\displaystyle f_{2}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\eta T}+C_{2}\eta+0+C_{4}\sqrt{p}+\frac{C_{5}\eta}{\sqrt{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{C}T}\right)+C_{2}\eta_{A}+0+\frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{E_{C}}}+C_{5}\eta_{C}\sqrt{2E_{C}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{A}T}+C_{2}\eta_{A}\right)}_{2M_{1}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}}{\eta_{C}T}+\frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{E_{C}}}+C_{5}\eta_{C}\sqrt{2E_{C}}\right)}_{3M_{3}}

\displaystyle=2M_{1}+3M_{3}.

For
Family 3
, substituting
\eta=\min\{\eta_{A},\eta_{C},\eta_{D}\},p=\frac{1}{E}=\frac{1}{\min\{\lceil E_{C}\rceil,\lceil E_{D}\rceil\}}=\frac{1}{\lceil\min\{E_{C},E_{D}\}\rceil}
,
and noting that
\min\{E_{C},E_{D}\}\geq 1
when
T\geq\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{1}C_{5}}{C_{4}^{2}},2\left(\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{6}}{C_{4}^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}
therefore
E_{C}\leq\lceil E_{C}\rceil<E_{C}+1\leq 2E_{C}
,
E_{D}\leq\lceil E_{D}\rceil<E_{D}+1\leq 2E_{D}
,
and using
\eta\leq\eta_{A},\frac{1}{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{A}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{C}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{D}},\sqrt{p}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{E}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{E_{C}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{E_{D}}},\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{p}}\leq\eta_{C}\cdot\sqrt{2E_{C}},\frac{\eta^{2}}{p}\leq\eta_{D}^{2}\cdot 2E_{D}
,
we have:

\displaystyle f_{3}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}

\displaystyle=f_{2}(\eta,p)_{C_{3}=0}+\frac{C_{6}\eta^{2}}{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{1}+3M_{3}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{C_{1}/2}{\eta_{D}T}+\frac{C_{1}/2}{\eta_{D}T}+\frac{C_{4}/2}{\sqrt{E_{D}}}+\frac{C_{4}/2}{\sqrt{E_{D}}}+2C_{6}\eta_{D}^{2}E_{D}\right)}_{5M_{4}}

\displaystyle=2M_{1}+3M_{3}+5M_{4}.

∎

D.4
Iteration Complexity with Logarithmic Dependence.

For
\varepsilon>0,\delta>0
, and any
q>0
, choosing

T\geq\frac{\mathrm{e}^{q^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\log^{q}\left[\mathrm{e}\vee\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\right]\implies\frac{\log^{q}(T/\delta)}{T}\leq\varepsilon

.

Proof.

From the premise, we multiply both sides by
\varepsilon
, take the
1/q
-th power,
and isolate the maximum operator to obtain

\log\left[\mathrm{e}\vee\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\right]=\max\left(1,\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{-q}(T\varepsilon)^{1/q}

The above inequality guarantee implies
T\varepsilon\geq\mathrm{e}^{q^{2}}
.
Because the maximum of the function
f(x)=\frac{\log x}{x^{1/q}}
is bounded by
1-\mathrm{e}^{-q}

for any
x\geq\mathrm{e}^{q^{2}}
, we have:

\log(T\varepsilon)\leq(1-\mathrm{e}^{-q})(T\varepsilon)^{1/q}

By leveraging
y\leq\max(1,y)
, we can safely absorb any values of
\varepsilon\delta
and evaluate the logarithmic terms in a single, unbroken inequality chain:

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{T}{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\right)+\log(T\varepsilon)\leq\max\left(1,\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon\delta}\right)+\log(T\varepsilon)\leq(T\varepsilon)^{1/q}

Since both sides are strictly positive, we raise the entire inequality to the power of
q
:

\log^{q}\left(\frac{T}{\delta}\right)\leq T\varepsilon
, dividing both sides by
T
completes the proof:

\frac{\log^{q}(T/\delta)}{T}\leq\varepsilon
.
∎

Appendix E
Application 1: Stochastic Optimization with Mirror Descent

E.1 Theorem of First-Order Stationarity for Stochastic Optimization

E.1
First-Order Stationarity for Stochastic Optimization with Mirror Descent.

Consider solving (

1
), where
\inf_{w\in\mathcal{W}}f(w)>-\infty
, via mirror descent
w_{t+1}=w_{t}-\eta_{t}P(w_{t},U_{t},\eta_{t})
using the normalized gradient estimator
U_{t}=v_{t}/\|v_{t}\|_{*}
. We establish the oracle and iteration complexities required to achieve
\varepsilon
-stationarity (
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\nabla f(w_{t})\|_{\ast}\|P_{t}\|^{2}\leq\varepsilon
) with probability at least
1-\delta
under various configurations of the correction term, momentum
\beta_{t}

p_{t}
. To highlight structural similarities, we extract a shared

,
3.2
,
5.1
